<meta>This page follows the title page of Caspar Stiller’s "Spanish Mirror" (1685). It contains a full-page allegorical engraving, a common feature in 17th-century intellectual works intended to signal the book’s philosophical or "hermetic" depth to the reader.</meta>

# [Frontispiece Illustration]

->An Allegorical Figure of Wisdom<-

This full-page engraving depicts a bearded male figure, likely representing <term>Hermes Trismegistus<note>A legendary Hellenistic figure considered the "thrice-great" author of the Hermetic Corpus; he was viewed as the patron of alchemy and ancient wisdom.</note></term> or a similar sage. He is dressed in heavy, layered robes and wears a distinctive tall, veiled headpiece. He stands upon rocky, uneven ground, suggesting the difficult path of knowledge.

In his raised right hand, he holds a long, thin staff that ends in a bright flame. A bird—perhaps an <term>eagle<note>Often a symbol of the volatile spirit or the process of sublimation in alchemy.</note></term> or a <term>phoenix<note>A mythical bird signifying rebirth and the successful completion of the alchemical "Great Work."</note></term>—is perched upon the staff. 

Suspended from the staff is a <term>cucurbit<note>A round-bottomed glass flask used as the base of a still in alchemical experiments.</note></term>. Inside this glass vessel, a small <term>serpent<note>In this context, a serpent or dragon forming a circle often represents the Ouroboros, symbolizing the unity of matter and the eternal cycle of nature.</note></term> is visible, curled in a circular shape.

The background features a detailed landscape: 
* To the left, a fortified castle or manor sits atop a grassy hill.
* In the center, a stone arch bridge spans a river. 
* To the right are several houses or buildings. 

Above the scene, the sky is filled with large, stylized, billowing clouds, rendered with fine hatching to create depth.

<meta>This page marks the beginning of the author's preface (Vorrede). The signature mark ") ( 3" indicates it is the third leaf of the introductory section. The catchword "ver-" suggests the next page begins with a word like "verachten" (despise) or "verbeatern" (improve).</meta>

# PREFACE
## to the Reader.

Gracious Reader, I could have well remained silent instead of setting down this modest work upon these few sheets. Since many better and more distinguished writings by the old *Philosophical Chemists* <note>original Latin: "Phil. Chymicis", referring to the alchemists of antiquity and the Middle Ages</note> are already found recorded, I might have abstained. However, my *affection* <note>or devotion</note> toward every lover of the art of true *Chemistry* <note>original Latin: "Chymia", which at this time encompassed both laboratory chemistry and speculative alchemy</note> would not allow me to keep silent; instead, I wished to gladly share as much as the highest Giver <note>A common reference to God as the source of all wisdom and natural secrets</note> has bestowed upon me. Although it is not explained through ornate and flowery language, I provide it as well as my ability allows: Whoever knows something better and

<summary>In this preface, the author humbly introduces his short treatise on alchemy, explaining that his desire to share divinely granted knowledge with fellow enthusiasts outweighed his hesitation to add to the existing body of scholarly work.</summary>
<keywords>preface, alchemy, philosophical chemists, chemistry, humility, divine knowledge, chemical art</keywords>

is able, let him also allow his neighbor to enjoy it; for I do not boast of being a highly learned scholar, but rather remain within my simplicity and *experiments*. For it is much better when one has performed a trial and then writes the truth about it; in this way, no one will easily be led astray <note>original: "hinter das Licht geführet," literally "led behind the light," a common idiom for being deceived</note>. Everyone now wishes to claim for themselves the *title* of a *chemist* <note>original Latin: "Chymici"</note>, and yet very few understand the use and property of the word *Chemistry* <note>original Latin: "Chimia"</note>, much less live according to it. There is soon hardly a barber-surgeon <term>Bader: a practitioner of minor surgery, bloodletting, and dentistry, often looked down upon by university-trained physicians</term>, craftsman, nor old toothless hag, nor other rabble-folk to be found who do not venture to take up *laboratory-work* <note>original: "Laboriren"</note>; these people completely obscure this noble science and bring it into contempt. Those people think that if they snatch up a *philosophical alchemist’s or chemical book*, they would be certain of the Work <note>The "Great Work" or Magnum Opus, the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> and could not fail: But it is in vain. So it is yet easy to understand / that
the alchemists <term>Alchymisten: practitioners of alchemy, often viewed in this period as "midwives" to nature who help it reach perfection</term> do not change the created nature
of God in and of itself and
out of its own powers / but
only come to its aid through
our Art <term>Kunſt: the technical and spiritual skill of alchemy</term> and experience / when we
perhaps take for one or
another imperfect metal
an addition <term>Zuſatz: a substance, often called a "ferment," added to a base metal to trigger its transformation into a higher state</term> of another perfect one /
which is then within nature /
and well permitted / for the Art
can indeed come to the aid of Nature /
as can be seen in waters and wines /
which one can elevate and improve /
through some addition of a sweet
and powerful thing / and yet
the nature of the water and the
wine remains: So also remains
the nature of quicksilver <term>Queckſilber: mercury, the primary fluid element in alchemical theory</term> / of copper /
tin and lead / although an ex-
perienced alchemist, through an addition
and his artistic fire, can elevate
the copper / tin and lead into silver /
or the silver into gold /
which is that person’s experience <note>original: "Erfahrung," here meaning the practical, proven knowledge gained through laboratory experimentation</note>.

...manifold [reasons] but left behind / which carries the title / *The Art of Transmutation* <note>original Latin: "Ars Transmutatoria"</note>, and I call myself without hesitation a *Chemist* <note>original: "Chimicum"</note>, which is a name I claim from the word *Chemistry* <note>original: "Chymia"</note>. This word signifies as much as making something Pure / Clear / freeing it from all evil, clinging matter / separating the good from the bad / making the fixed volatile / and the volatile fixed <note>These are core alchemical concepts: "fixing" a substance means making it resistant to fire, while making it "volatile" means allowing it to vaporize or transform</note> : But / there are many impertinent wiseacres <note>original: "naseweise Klüglinge," a derogatory term for those who pretend to have deep knowledge but lack true understanding</note> who, whenever they hear of a *Medicine* <note>original: "Medicament"</note> which a sensible *Chemist* <note>original: "Chymicus"</note> has prepared / cry out that it is poison. In this, they act against the Truth / and thus many must suffer innocently who mean well by their neighbors; it is the way of the world <note>original: "Welt-Lauff"</note> / and as long as the world stands / it will not be otherwise. Be that as it may / the Truth will always prevail. For we [chemists] do nothing other / than what our own stomach performs within our body / which, by God’s ordinance, receives food /

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page begins the main body of the text. The signature "A" marks the start of the first section of the book. The text transitions from a theological justification of health to a practical classification of natural materials. The catchword "den/" at the bottom anticipates the word "werden" (to be/become) on the following page.</meta>

God the Lord has created three things for human health, which are called the three kingdoms of the world. First, there is the *vegetable kingdom* <note>original Latin: "Vegetabile"</note>, which consists of roots, herbs, flowers, seeds, etc. The second kingdom is called the *animal kingdom* <note>original Latin: "Animale"</note>, which consists of animals; whatever names they may have, they nonetheless serve human health. The third consists of the *mineral kingdom* <note>original Latin: "Minerali"</note>, from which metals are smelted; these also serve health—indeed, even more than the other two—for those who recognize the true star-fluid flower <note>original: "Stern-flüssige Blume." This is a cryptic alchemical term likely referring to a "celestial" essence or a specific purified state of matter believed to be influenced by the stars</note>. I will take my beginning from the easiest and 1. consider the *vegetable*, 2. treat the *animal*, and 3. the *mineral* and its mercury <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note>.

Regarding the first *matter* <note>original Latin: "materia," referring here to the "materia prima" or fundamental building blocks of the plants</note> of vegetables, it consists of water and earth, which lie hidden in their seeds, and in which more water than earth is found. This now exists in various effects and qualities, and can, through the <term>Artisan's</term> <note>original Latin: "Artificis"; in this context, the "Artisan" is the chemist or alchemist who knows how to manipulate nature</note> hand and skill, be brought back to—

Such an <term>error</term> <note>original: "Irzthumb"</note> is still very much in vogue today, especially among those driven by greed. These people take a herb—it matters little to them when it was gathered (I speak not of all, but of many)—and from it they distil twenty to thirty pitchers of liquid using good well-water. From this they gain much money; though the herb itself did not even cost two <term>Mariengroschen</term> <note>original: "Mgr."; a small silver coin used in northern Germany</note>, they earn several <term>Florins</term> <note>original: "fl."; a high-value gold or silver currency</note> from the sale.

If one examines such a distilled water to see what benefit it might provide (even though the learned <term>Physicians</term> <note>original Latin: "Medici"</note> rely upon them), and what has actually been carried over in the <term>distillation</term>, one finds nothing other than the smell. This scent comes from the herb's oil; and although a little of the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original Latin: "Spiritu"; the volatile, active essence of the plant</note> of the herb is mixed in, it is so very little within that great quantity of water that it can be of little use to the sick. Far less can it take away their ailment. 

Indeed, many a patient—who already finds <term>medicines</term> <note>original: "Artzneyen"</note> loathsome to take—is greatly martyred by these large draughts, so that he desires to die rather than to live. God the **Lord** does not look upon the misery of the patient with mercy <note>The author implies that using poor-quality medicine is a sign that divine favor is absent from the treatment</note>. If one views nature correctly, one must surely agree with me that no substance can be "opened up" <note>An alchemical term for breaking down a substance to release its inner virtues</note> with common water, much less can the impure be separated from the pure. Just as in the practice of a *distillation* <note>original Latin: "distillatio"</note> where a water comes over [the helm], the *essential salt* <note>original Latin: "Sal essentiale"; the mineral salts of the plant which contain its medicinal power</note> remains behind in the *residue* <note>original Latin: "residuo"</note>. This they then cast away, taking the shadow instead of the body.

There are sometimes wise-acres who *strain* <note>original: "coliren"</note> the residue and *thicken* <note>original: "inspissiren"; to evaporate a liquid into a syrup or paste</note> it together with the "bound earth" <note>the solid, inactive sediment</note> in the name of an *Extract*. They do this only so they may have their profit and fill their empty jars. For otherwise, according to proper use as recorded in the *dispensatory* <note>original Latin: "dispensatorio"; an official handbook of medicinal recipes</note>, it should be made with *Spirit of Wine* <note>original Latin: "Spiritu Vini"; high-proof alcohol used as a solvent</note>. And even if it is prepared *according to the art* <note>original Latin: "secundùm artem"; following the correct professional standards</note>, a good grain brandy must do the best it can.

Those people only think: if it is an "extract," it may help however it can. But what actually happens when your *medicine* <note>original: "Medicament"</note> is *administered* <note>original: "adhibirt"</note>? What benefit does it bring? Consider your conscience! Though indeed, the conscience is often so "narrow" that a wagon-load of hay can drive right into it <note>A sarcastic German proverb meaning the person has a very loose or non-existent conscience</note>. This medicine is supposed to help against vomiting, yet it causes it all the more. Why? Because the *calx* <note>original Latin: "Calc."; mineral residue or metallic oxide</note> from the copper still is *extracted* along with it.

5
A iij

...the **essential** <note>original: "effentiale"; the vital spirit or quintessence of the plant</note> takes up into the heights with it, and serves for the benefit and strength of man.

### The process is thus:

One should take a herb—whichever one is desired—at the time it **blooms** <note>original: "floriret"</note> in its proper month, early before the sun rises while it is still moistened with dew. Collect it in a great quantity (herbs in May are the best for this). Chop it finely and fill a large glass **flask** <note>original: "Kolben"; a round-bottomed vessel used in distillation</note> with it. **Lute** <note>original: "lutir"; to seal the joints of the apparatus with a paste or clay to prevent vapors from escaping</note> a **still-head** <note>original: "Helm"; the top part of the still that directs vapor to the condenser</note> onto it with a suitable **receiver** <note>original: "Vorlage"; the vessel that catches the condensed liquid</note>. Place the flask into a **water bath** <note>original: "Balneum"; also known as a bain-marie, used for gentle, indirect heating</note> so that it may **macerate** <note>original: "macerire"; to soak or soften to extract flavors or chemicals</note> for 14 days, providing a very gentle fire.

After these days have ended, increase the fire and continue to **distill** <note>original: "distillire"</note> until something begins to pass over. Remove the flask from the **water bath** <note>original: "Balneo"</note>, and its own juice will have revealed itself. **Press out** <note>original: "exprimir"</note> the juice. Take the **residue** <note>original: "remanenz"</note> of the herb and dry it; afterward, burn it to an ash. Extract the **salt** <note>original: "Saltz"; in spagyric medicine, the mineral salts of the plant represent its "body" and are essential for a complete medicine</note> from the ash using its own distilled water. **Crystallize** <note>original: "congelir"</note> it until it is pure, and mix it with its own juice. 

Place this back into a convenient distillation flask and, as previously mentioned, let it **macerate** <note>original: "maceriren"</note> again for 14 days. Then begin to distill, and a green water will pass over. Pour this back onto what is in the flask and begin to distill once more; this **cohobation** <note>original: "cohobatio"; the process of repeatedly redistilling a liquid over its solid base to concentrate its virtues</note> re-

The **spirit** that was drawn off is **impregnated** <note>saturated</note> with the salt of the herb and is much better than a **spirit** that merely bears a herb's name—as when one pours common **grain brandy** <note>original: "Korn-Brandwein"</note> over the herb and draws it off again through **distillation**. Such a preparation provides as much benefit as a common brandy, only that it carries a flavor [of the herb], so that it is falsely and improperly called the **spirit** of the herb. One can now clearly see how such an **extract** or **essence** is better than the common ones made with poor brandy. ### Concerning Minerals. <note>original: "De Mineralibus"</note>

**As** concerns their **origin**, they consist of a slimy **water** and a twofold **earth**, which we call **Pure** and **Impure**. The impure **earth** is called the combustible **sulfur** <note>original: "**Schwefel**"; in alchemical theory, sulfur represented the principle of combustibility and color.</note>, which prevents the **casting** <note>The ability of a metal to be melted and poured.</note> and gives only a slight **form** to the **water** added to it, as can be seen in the lesser **minerals**, such as **antimony** <note>original: "*Antimon*"</note>, **marcasite** <note>original: "*Marcasita*"; a term often used for iron pyrites or other metallic-looking minerals.</note>, **magnesia** <note>original: "*Magnesia*"; in this period, it could refer to various minerals including manganese or varieties of magnetite.</note>, and the like. But a pure **earth** is bound with its aforementioned **water**, which no **force** of the **fire** can separate, for they either both flee away from it, or else remain stable within it.

<meta>This page continues the transition from general mineralogy into the specific study of metallic principles. It follows the previous discussion on how "pure" and "impure" earths combine with water to form the basis of all minerals. This page specifically introduces the "Sulfur-Mercury theory," a cornerstone of medieval and Renaissance alchemy which posited that all metals are composed of these two principles in varying degrees of purity and "coction" (cooking).</meta>

From such a slimy **water** and liquid **earth** or **sulfur**, that same substance is composed which is called **quicksilver** <note>original: "**Quecksilber**"</note>, namely the first **matter** <note>original: "*Materia*"</note> of all **metals**. And the **metals** are nothing else than a cooked **mercury** <note>original: "*Mercurius*"</note>, cooked through various **heat**. This different **action** of **heat** causes in the **composite** <note>original: "*composito*"</note> of the **metal** both its **ripeness** and **unripeness** <note>Alchemists believed metals "grew" in the earth like fruit; gold was "ripe," while lead was "unripe."</note>. From this it is now to be concluded and follows that the slimy **water**, when combined with its subtle, pure **earth**, brings about **quicksilver**. From this substance, subsequently according to the **opportunity** of different **dispositions** <note>original: "*Disposition*"</note> and attained **properties** of some heat (also by means of natural **heat**), different **metals** arise. **Why** I explain this, everyone will well see, and what I mean by it; I will therefore proceed further and make the **beginning** with **quicksilver**, also thereby proving that it is the first **matter** <note>original: "*Materia*"</note> of all **metals**.

**Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> is the **First Matter** <note>original: *Materia*; the primordial substance from which all things are made</note>, according to the testimony of the true **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophen*; here referring to alchemists</note> in the first discourse of the ***Turba*** <note>original: *Turb.*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers," one of the earliest and most influential Latin alchemical texts</note> in the 1st Chapter.

...rather those who do not understand any better, nor wish to understand, what the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring to alchemical authorities</note> mean in their metaphorical words or writings. Here, one does not mean the **Mercury of the common people** <note>original: *Mercur. vulgi*; ordinary liquid quicksilver</note>, but rather the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*; a refined, spiritualized substance central to the Great Work</note>. Should one conduct a close examination of the **Mercury of the common people** to see if it is suitable for making an internally administered medicine, it is indeed something, yet very dangerous, because no proper **preparation** <note>original: *præparation*; the chemical process of refining or purifying a substance</note> is undertaken with it.

Those commonly in use, which are made from ordinary quicksilver—such as ***Precipitated Mercury***, ***Cosmetic Mercury***, ***Sweet Mercury*** <note>original: *Mercurius Dulcis*; also known as calomel, a common but toxic purgative</note>, ***Mercury of Life*** <note>original: *Mercurius Vitæ*; a preparation of antimony, not actually mercury</note>, ***Cinnabar of Antimony***, ***Mineral Bezoar*** <note>original: *Bezoart. mineral.*; a compound used to induce sweating</note>, etc.—indeed do their part. And because many a person has no better **medications** <note>original: *Medicamenta*</note>, he sticks with them; however, no one can confess with a good conscience that they truly benefit nature.

But whoever observes nature closely and investigates diligently finds that what has not been dissolved and separated, **the pure from the impure** <note>original: *purum ab impuro*</note>, brings no fruit <note>benefit or result</note> in the human body. Therefore, those who desire to work in nature could accomplish everything with a single **medication** <note>original: *Medicamento*</note> if they had drawn from the pure fountain out of our heavenly ***Chaos*** <term>Chaos: in alchemy, this refers to the primordial, unformed matter or "First Matter" which contains the potential for all things</term>; but there are few who attain such a thing.

<summary>This page distinguishes between common quicksilver and the "Mercury of the Philosophers," warning that common mercury-based medicines are dangerous if not properly purified. <meta>This page continues the discussion from page 23 regarding the origins of metals. On the previous page, the author cited the *Turba Philosophorum* and Arnoldus de Villa Nova to establish that all metals are essentially "thickened mercury." This page provides a logical proof for that claim using an analogy of ice and water, and introduces the testimony of Bernard of Trevisan.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>
-> ❦ 24 ❦ <-

In this, the *Philosophers* <note>original: *Philosophi*; the traditional authorities on alchemy and natural science</note> all agree: **Ice** or **snow** is turned back into **water** by means of heat; therefore, it was **water** before it became **snow** or **ice**. Likewise, all *metals* <note>original: *Metalla*</note> are turned back into *mercury* <note>original: *Mercurius*</note>; therefore, they were also *mercury* beforehand. *Trevisanus* <note>Bernard of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemist known for his quest for the Philosopher's Stone</note> is of the same opinion in his book on the transformation of metals, where he says: *Quicksilver* <note>original: *Argentum vivum*</note> is the first *matter* <note>original: *Materia*; the fundamental substance</note> of all metals, and it is like a **water** for the sake of the uniformity <note>or "likeness"</note> it shares with *plants* <note>original: *Vegetabilibus*</note> and *animals* <note>original: *Animalibus*</note>. And it takes onto itself the power of the things which are attached to it during its decoction <note>the process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid</note>. Just so, he says, **gold** is nothing other than a *mercury* congealed through the power of its *sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphuris*</note>. Just as nature miraculously makes **gold** from *mercury* in a simple manner, so **gold** is nothing other than a *perfected mercury* <note>original: *Merc. anatizatus*; likely from a Greek root meaning "thoroughly cooked" or "perfected"</note>, that is, uniformly cooked within the interior of a mineral earth. And the *Philosophers* have indicated that this happens through the touch of *sulfur*, which *coagulates* <note>solidifies</note> the *mercury*; and by cause of its action—that is, from the *mercury* which is *digested* <note>original: *digeriret*; a process of slow, consistent heating</note> and made thick through uniform warmth—
<meta>catchword: therefore (daher)</meta>

<summary>The author presents a logical proof that all metals originate from mercury by comparing them to ice returning to water. He cites Bernard of Trevisan to explain that gold is simply mercury that has been perfected and solidified by sulfur and natural heat within the earth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the critique of "false" alchemists from page 29. The previous page ended mid-sentence, describing how improper heat creates a "fever" in the substance; this page completes that thought, warning of the "irrecoverable error" that follows.</meta>

...forth an irrecoverable error and vain work will arise. Fools make and extract **sharp waters** <note>original: *scharffe Wasser*; a term for corrosive acids like nitric or sulfuric acid</note> from minor minerals; into these they throw a kind of metal and let them be consumed: For they think that [the metals] are immediately dissolved by a natural *solution* <note>original: *solution*; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid</note>, whereas such a *solution* actually requires and demands that the *solvens* <note>original: *solvens*; the dissolving agent or solvent</note> and that which is *solviret* <note>original: *solviret*; dissolved</note> should remain together; for from both—namely, the **male and female seeds**—a new *Species* and kind arises. 

Truly I say to you, that no water can *dissolve* a metallic kind through natural restoration, unless it remains in them in *Materia* <note>original: *Materia*; the physical substance or "stuff" of the work</note> and *Form* <note>original: *Form*; the essential character or "soul" of the substance</note>, and which can again *coagulate* <note>original: *coaguliren*; to thicken or solidify from a liquid state</note> the *solviret* metals; this cannot happen in **strong water** <note>original: *starcken Wasser*; usually refers to *aqua fortis* (nitric acid), which dissolves metals but is difficult to "re-solidify" back into the original metallic nature</note>, but is rather a corruption of the *Compositi* <note>original: *Compositi*; the chemical composite or mixture</note> as well as the *Corporis* <note>original: *Corporis*; the physical "body" of the metal</note> that is meant to be *dissolved*. Furthermore, a water does not belong to those bodies in the act of dissolving if it does not remain with them during *coagulation* or thickening; such a [water], however, is the *Mercur.* <note>original: *Mercur.*; Mercury or Quicksilver</note> and not the strong waters, or what the ignorant call a *Mercurial.* water.

<meta>This page continues the critique of alchemists who use improper methods to dissolve metals. The previous page discussed how strong acids corrupt the metallic "body" rather than truly dissolving it for rebirth; this page specifically warns against destroying the "spermatic nature" of mercury by over-processing it into a simple water.</meta>

Those are also mistaken who are of the opinion that they can **extract** a clear, transparent water from **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*; in alchemy, this refers both to the element quicksilver and the philosophical principle of fluidity and transformation</note>, and wish to make many wonderful things from it. Even supposing that they could produce such a water, such a work would be of no use, nor suited to its nature and **proportion** <note>the specific balance of elements required for a successful reaction</note>; it cannot restore [the substance] nor bring about a perfect metallic **Species** <note>original: *Specie*; a distinct, perfected type of metal</note>.

For as soon as **Mercury** is transformed from its original nature, it is forbidden from entering into our **Philosophical Work** <note>the *Magnum Opus* or the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, because it has lost its **Spermatic Nature** <note>original: *Spermatische Natur*; the internal "seed" or life-force believed to allow metals to grow and transmute</note> and the essential property of metals. Yet I am not against an **accidental** cleaning <note>original: *accidentalische Reinigung*; a superficial or outward purification that does not change the core essence</note> being performed upon it, according to proper **Philosophical Experience**, in order to take away the outward dross <note>original: *Schlacken*; waste matter or impurities</note> and mineral filth.

Such cleaning happens by means of our **flying eagles** <note>original: *fliegende Adler*; an alchemical metaphor usually referring to volatile substances like sal ammoniac or the process of sublimation</note> with a special **central salt** <note>original: *centralischen Saltze*; a salt thought to contain the essential "center" or fire of a substance</note>; by this, the **Mercurial Form** <note>the essential soul or identity of the mercury</note> is not taken away, but rather improved thereby. Therefore, one must diligently guard against and watch every **Solution** <note>the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note>, so that the **Matter** is not allowed to become glassy <note>original: *glasicht*; referring to vitrification, a state where the alchemist believed the "life" of the metal was permanently locked away and killed</note>. See now, you who [pursue] such...

<summary>The author warns that over-refining mercury into a "clear water" destroys its generative "spermatic" power, rendering it useless for the Great Work. He allows for superficial purification using "flying eagles" (volatile agents) but cautions against letting the matter turn into a lifeless glass.</summary>

<keywords>mercury, philosophical work, spermatic nature, purification, flying eagles, central salt, solution, vitrification, alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the warning against improper alchemical methods. The previous page discussed how the use of volatile substances like sal ammoniac can lead to the destruction of the metal's internal "seed" if not handled with philosophical care. This page specifically addresses the error of using corrosive acids, which the author views as destructive rather than transformative.</meta>

...hence an irrecoverable error and vain work will arise. The fools make and **extract** <term>extrahiren: to draw out a specific substance through distillation or chemical action</term> sharp waters <note>original: *scharffe Wasser*; these are strong mineral acids, such as nitric acid (aqua fortis), which dissolve metals by corroding them</note> from common minerals, throw a type of metal into them, and let them be consumed. 

For they think that the metals are immediately **dissolved** <term>solviret</term> through a natural **solution** <term>solution: the process of turning a solid "body" into a liquid "spirit"</term>. Yet such a **solution** requires and demands that the **solvent** <term>solvens: the substance that performs the dissolving</term> and that which is **dissolved** should remain together; for from both—namely, the **Male and Female Seed** <note>the alchemical belief that metals possess gendered principles (usually Sulfur as male and Mercury as female) that must unite to create the "offspring" or the Stone</note>—a new **Species** <note>a new, perfected form of matter</note> and type arises. 

Truly I tell you, no water can **dissolve** <term>dissolviren</term> a metallic type through natural restoration, unless that water remains within them in **Matter** <term>Materia</term> and **Form** <term>Form</term>, and is able to **coagulate** <term>coaguliren: the process of a liquid thickening or returning to a solid state</term> the dissolved metals again. This cannot happen in "strong water" <note>corrosive acid</note>; rather, such acid causes a corruption of the **Composite** <note>original: *Compositi*; the combined alchemical substance</note> and of the **Body** <note>original: *Corporis*; the physical substance of the metal</note> that is meant to be dissolved. 

Furthermore, a water does not belong to those bodies during the process of dissolving if it does not remain with them during the **coagulation** or thickening. Such a substance [that remains] is **Mercury**, and not the "strong waters," or what the ignorant call a **Mercurial Water** <note>the author distinguishes between literal quicksilver or acid and the "Philosophical Mercury," which is the only substance capable of a true, creative union with metals</note>.

<summary>The author argues that using corrosive acids to dissolve metals is a mistake because these "strong waters" destroy the metal's essence; true alchemical dissolution requires a solvent (mercury) that stays united with the metal to allow for its later rebirth.</summary>

Those are also in error who are of the opinion that they can **extract** <term>extrahiren: to draw out a specific substance through distillation or chemical action</term> a clear, transparent water from **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note> and want to make many wonderful things from it. For as soon as the **Mercury** <note>the liquid metal quicksilver, considered the "mother" of all metals</note> is transformed from its original nature, it is forbidden from entering our **Philosophical Work** <note>the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, because it has lost its **spermatic nature** <note>the generative, life-giving power contained within the "seed" of the metal</note> and the properties of the metals. 

Yet I am not against an **accidental purification** <note>original: *accidentalische Reinigung*; a cleaning of the external or superficial impurities rather than a fundamental change of the substance’s essence</note> being performed on it, but only according to proper philosophical experience, in order to remove the external **slags** <term>Schlacken: waste matter separated from metals during smelting</term> and **mineral filth**.

By this, the **Mercurial Form** <note>the essential "soul" or identity of the mercury</note> is not taken away, but rather improved.

Or **suppose** <note>original Latin: "positò"</note> that it were dissolved and nothing remained on the bottom; yet the metal does not stay joined with the **solvent** <note>original Latin: "solvente"</note>. Instead, if the solvent is merely **evaporated** <note>original: "evaporiret"</note>, or if only **Tartarus** <term>Tartarus: Potassium bitartrate (cream of tartar), frequently used in early chemistry to cause metals to drop out of a liquid solution</term> is added, then this same metal falls back to the bottom again. To prove this further: once a transient **process-monger** <term>Process-Kramer: A derogatory term for a traveling peddler who sold fraudulent or unverified alchemical recipes ("processes") to gullible patrons</term> came to me. He prattled to me of great, magnificent works; but because I had investigated Nature more deeply, I paid no mind to his talk. Yet, I let him say whatever he wished. This "good fellow" saw clearly that he could achieve nothing with me, yet he still wished to appear respectful and presented me with a **particular tincture** <term>Particular Tincture: An alchemical preparation intended to transform only a specific amount of base metal into gold or silver, rather than the "Universal" stone which was said to transform infinite amounts</term>. He swore a solemn oath that it worked. He claimed he had not made it himself, but had **promoted** <note>original: "promoviret"; in this context, it means he provided or sold the secret to a patron</note> it to someone in England who possessed very great wealth. I accepted it and tested it soon after; then an **Oil of Mars** <term>Oil of Mars (Oleum Martis): A caustic liquid made from iron (associated with the planet Mars), usually iron chloride</term> made itself known, and it was merely the **procedure** <note>original: "Proc."</note> for Oil of Mars.

thus: **Take iron filings** <term>Rafur. Where there is **Salt**, there is also **Sulfur** and **Mercury**. <note>original: *Merc.*; shorthand for *Mercurius*</note> Where **Mercury** is, there is also **Sulfur** and **Salt**; and where **Sulfur** is, there is also **Salt** and **Mercury**; for these three are one **in essence** <note>original: *in Essentia*</note> and each is always the other's **magnet** <note>In alchemy, a "magnet" refers to the natural attraction between substances of the same root</note>. They do not **differ in reality** <note>original: *differiren nicht realiter*</note>, but rather **by reason of their different qualities and operations** <note>original: *ratione diversarum qualitatum & operationum*</note>. They are simple in their being and threefold in their functions; they can, therefore, not be separated from one another.

One sees, for this reason, how a **philosophical solvent** <note>original: *Philosophisches solvens*</note> and **solute** <note>original: *solutum*</note> made with **Mercury**—wherein the **entire substance has been reduced to the first matter** <term>First Matter: *Prima Materia*; the primitive, formless base of all matter which alchemists sought to isolate as the starting point for the Great Work</term>—is of a different nature than the common **extractions of tinctures**. For ours "lie" or "fly" together <note>A reference to the chemical properties of "fixity" and "volatility." The author claims his preparation is so perfectly unified that the components will either stay together in the heat or evaporate together as one</note>. This is how it happens with a natural **solution** <note>original: *Solution*</note>: only like joins with like. There, the **active and passive elements** <note>original: *agens & patiens*</note> must stem from one root if they are to **remain together in the same subject** <note>original: *in eodem subjecto subsistiren*</note>. **Therefore, Art imitates Nature; proceed in accordance with Nature, and Nature will yield to what is natural.** <note>original: *Ergo Ars imitari Naturam; Naturæ convenienter procede, & Naturæ naturæ convenienti cedet.* This is a core alchemical tenet: the chemist is merely Nature's assistant and must follow her rules to succeed.</note>

Thus, it is sufficiently proven and demonstrated what exists within **Mercury**, and also how one should behave regarding a **philosophical solvent**. However, my faithful spirit leads me toward those lovers of the Art who seek a...

...seek **true medicine**, so that they may in the future learn from me how a **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercur.*</note> can be made from every mineral, as well as a true **oil** <note>original: *ol.*; shorthand for *oleum*</note> which is sweet and contains not the slightest bit of **corrosive** <note>original: *corosiv*; refers to caustic or acid substances that "eat" through metal or flesh</note>. However, I promise to gladly and willingly assist any **lover of Nature** <note>A common term for a dedicated student of alchemy or natural philosophy</note> who desires this of me.

And so I will let the matter rest for now, hoping that everyone will find satisfaction in it. I turn now to the remaining **metals**, to consider them briefly, and to make a beginning with **Gold**. It has already been demonstrated in the previous discussion on **mercury** <note>original: *mercury*</note> what the metals are and from what they are created.

As for **Gold**, it is the heaviest and most **fixed** <term>Fixed: *fixeste*; in alchemy, "fixity" refers to a substance's ability to withstand the heat of fire without evaporating or changing</term> metal. How to bring this into a good **medicine** <note>original: *medicament*</note> has already been discussed; yet, without achieving a true **drinkability** <term>Potability: *potabilität*; referring to *Aurum Potabile* or "Drinkable Gold," a legendary universal cure-all made by dissolving gold into a form the body can absorb</term>, it is all in vain to attempt anything within the human body. For gold cannot be overcome by **earthly fire**, much less should the **stomach** be expected to do so.

As for **Silver**, its quality follows closely after **Gold**. I would have reason enough to write much about it; however, since it is not my intention to introduce many unnecessary matters, and I prefer to embrace brevity: nothing better for health can be made from this metal than the **tincture** or **drinkable Silver** <note>original: *Luna potabil*; in alchemy, *Luna* (the Moon) is the name for Silver, just as *Sol* (the Sun) is Gold</note>. 

But I have seen very little of true silver tincture; that which is shown as blue in the pharmacies is not the correct kind, but rather only a "shadow of the body" <note>The author suggests the blue color is a superficial trait of the metal's "body" rather than its true medicinal essence</note>. By a single, true, and very distinguished **chemist** <note>original: *Laboranten*; one who performs laboratory work</note>, it was shown and gifted to me as having the appearance of blood. 

A blue tincture does not come from silver, but from **copper**. Should one take the **residue** <note>original: *residuum*; the material remaining after the tincture is extracted</note> from which the blue tincture was drawn and melt it with **tartar** <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate, a common alchemical flux</note>, he will recover his good silver again. To test this, melt your silver with **borax** <note>original: *Borrax*; a mineral salt used to help metals melt and to remove impurities</note>—specifically, 1 part silver to 2 parts borax—and you will find that the **slag** <note>the stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting</note> is entirely blue. Melt it a second time in a new **crucible** <note>original: *Tiegel*; a heat-resistant container used for melting metal</note> with fresh borax, and the slag will appear green; and having done this a third and fourth time, it will take on no color at all. Why? Because the **volatile sulfur** has joined itself to the **salt** <note>In the "Three Principles" theory, metals are composed of Sulfur, Salt, and Mercury. Here, the author explains that the impurities (copper) are being stripped away by the salt of the borax</note>.

Try your luck now at obtaining a blue **tincture** from such pure silver; but throw in just one grain of **copper**, and you will immediately get a blue **tincture**. Now you see the difference, if you are clever.

Concerning **Mars** <note>The alchemical name for Iron</note> and its properties, I can with good reason value it as noble, even though it is a coarse fellow, and one has much work to do in "chopping off its rough knots" <note>An alchemical metaphor for refining the metal and removing its crude, impure qualities</note>; nevertheless, it accomplishes much good, both in metals and in humans. ## On Vitriol <note>original: *De Vitriolo*; vitriol refers to a class of sulfate minerals, most commonly iron or copper sulfate, prized for making acidic "spirits"</note>.

In my previous [writings] regarding the proof of *Mercury* <note>original: *Mercur.*; here referring to "Philosophical Mercury," the universal principle of fluidity and transformation rather than common quicksilver</note>, which I called the *Prime Matter* <note>original: *Prima materia*; the original, undifferentiated substance from which all metals are thought to grow</note> / I have reflected and reported somewhat extensively / that everything consists in this / which the **Sages** <note>the alchemists of old</note> desire / and that one can dissolve all **metals** with it / which also love it dearly. On the other hand, the *solutions* <note>original: *Solutiones*</note> / made with sharp, corrosive **water** <note>referring to mineral acids like aqua fortis</note> / are of no use at all for *Medicine* / nor are they a *radical solution* <note>original: *radical Solutio*; a "root-level" dissolution where the metal is returned to its primary liquid state without being damaged by harsh acids</note> / for which reason the prepared **salts** / made with *Spirit of Vitriol, of Salt, and of Nitre* <note>these correspond to sulfuric, hydrochloric, and nitric acids</note> / are of no use. But what nature brings about in the earth /

## *On Niter* <note>original Latin title: *De Nitro*; refers to saltpeter (potassium nitrate)</note>

At the end of my modest little work, I cannot refrain from concluding with this miraculous salt. He who would be a true <term>chemist</term> <note>original: *Chimicus*</note> may rightly call it so, for it performs many wonders in <term>chemical operations</term> <note>original: *Chimischen operationibus*</note>. It is wonderfully created and has been changed from one essence into another. It is cold and contrary to fire, and yet it is a pure fire that can bring all metals into <term>flux</term> <note>original: *Fluß*; the state of being melted or made liquid</note>; the ancients gave it the name of the "Hellish <term>Cerberus</term>" <note>original: *Cerberum*; the three-headed dog of the underworld in Greek mythology, used here to describe the salt's aggressive chemical power</note>. <term>Brother Basil Valentine</term> <note>original: *Frater Basil. Valent.*; a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 17th century</note> calls it the "cold ore-serpent." I can name it in three ways: a <term>vegetable salt</term> <note>original Latin: *Sal Vegetab.*</note>, an <term>animal salt</term> <note>original Latin: *Sal Animal.*</note>, and a <term>mineral salt</term> <note>original Latin: *Sal Minerale*</note>, and yet it comes from a single source. Let us consider its origin for a moment, where it is most often found: it is found in the livestock stalls where sheep or similar cattle stand, and this salt is created through daily <term>soaking</term> <note>original: *imbibirung*; the process of a solid material, like soil or floor-dirt, absorbing a liquid, in this case, animal urine</note>

<summary>The author begins a concluding chapter on Niter (saltpeter), describing it as a paradoxical "miraculous salt" that acts as a cold fire to melt metals. He notes its alchemical aliases and explains that it forms in animal stalls through the absorption of fluids into the earth.</summary>

## Of This Small Booklet.

Favorable Reader, it would perhaps have been my duty at the beginning of this work to assist with the construction of costly **furnaces**, **lutes** <note>original Latin: *Luta*; sealing clays or pastes used to make airtight joints in laboratory glass</note>, and **chemical operations** <note>original: *Chymischen operationibus*</note>. To make the fire under such a furnace, one takes glowing coals and throws them down into the cross <note>This refers to the cross-shaped pit/channel described on the previous page which serves as the combustion chamber.</note>; afterward, one fills the tower <note>This "tower" functions as a fuel hopper, a characteristic feature of an Athanor furnace designed for constant heat.</note> completely with other unlit coals and seals it well at the top so that nothing steams out; thus, the fire burns only in the cross-channel and nowhere else, which means that one can maintain such a fire for 14 days without adding any fuel. One takes a tough clay <note>original: *Leimen*</note>, lets it dry in the sun, and grinds it into a fine powder. Pour in enough cattle blood and mix it into a thick paste <note>original: *Mueß*; literally a mush or pulp</note>. With this, you can coat your vessels as needed and let them dry well in the shade; they will then withstand the strongest fire.

## On Chemical Operations

There are indeed many chemical tasks, but the foremost among them are the following: namely distillation <note>original: *Destillatio*</note>, preparation <note>original: *Præparatio*</note>, calcination <note>original: *Calcinatio*; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter</note>, solution <note>original: *Solutio*; dissolving a solid into a liquid</note>, congelation <note>original: *Congelatio*; the thickening or solidification of a substance</note>, fixation <note>original: *Fixatio*; making a volatile substance stable so it does not evaporate when heated</note>, sublimation <note>original: *Sublimatio*; heating a solid until it turns to vapor and then collects again as a solid</note>, projection <note>original: *Projectio*; the final stage of an alchemical transformation, where a small amount of "medicine" or catalyst is cast upon a molten metal</note>, and others besides, through which everything is accomplished.

## The Chemical

### To all most gracious Lords.

I offer herewith my most humble service and duty, and my most bounden, obedient loyalty. Most gracious Prince, gracious Count, and Lords. It is well known and understood by you, my gracious, High-Princely, and High-born Lords, with what great diligence and high understanding every art and ingenious invention—which is useful and necessary for temporal welfare <note>original: "Wolfahrt"</note> and physical preservation <note>original: "Erhaltung"</note>—was first discovered by the dear ancients, our ancestors <note>original: "Vorfahren"</note>. In my earlier days, I was driven hither and thither by envy (just as I am still greatly hated now), and though I was nearly driven into total misery, I was taken in and accepted by Your High-Countly Graces. Through my small, dedicated book, I was favored with a respectable **appointment** <note>original: "Bestallung" — a formal commission or professional position, usually including a salary.</note>. To Your Serene Princely Highnesses and Your High-Countly Graces, however—to whom I remain bound in most humble obedience through my God-given sciences—I have intended to make my capability known by presenting this as a true **defense** <note>original: "Apologia"</note> of the **philosophers** <note>The author refers to natural philosophers or alchemists, who seek the hidden truths of the physical world.</note>, supported by **experiments** <note>original: "Experimenten"</note> conducted over time. And although no one can promise such **great works** <note>original: "Magnalia" — a Latin term for the "great things" or wonders of God and nature, often used in alchemy to describe significant discoveries like the Philosopher's Stone.</note> within a self-appointed time, the Giver of all good things holds His hand over it and knows well when the time is best. This is something Your Serene Princely Highnesses and Your High-Countly Graces have also experienced, and thus You will all the more readily place your trust in an untiring seeker of the **truth** <note>original: "Warheit"</note>.

I am also confident that no deceit or trickery will be suspected of my humble self, for I apply myself to these things for the glory of God and the benefit of my neighbor—specifically for the preservation of long-lasting **health** <note>original: *Geſundheit*</note>—and I shall persevere in this until my end. In zealous desire, I shall make it my highest priority to further the beloved **mining works** <note>original: *Berg-Wercks*; in this era, mining and alchemy were sister sciences, as both sought to understand the growth and refinement of metals within the earth.</note> and other useful endeavors, dedicating both my life and property to this cause, in good times and bad. 

Just as the dear God ignites a bright light for me from time to time, and continues to show me the way daily, I have most humbly presented this work as a well-founded foretaste of Divine goodness and the **knowledge of true wisdom** <note>original: *erkandtnuß der wahren Weißheit*</note>.

# PREFACE TO THE READER <note>original: *PRÆFATIO AD LECTOREM*</note>

<image-desc>A decorative horizontal woodcut border consisting of repeating stylized floral and foliage motifs arranged symmetrically.</image-desc>

Kind friend—whoever you may be—this well-meaning effort of mine, which I have now taken up the pen to prepare for print, has been done for this reason: so that I might allow you to catch a glimpse of the **treasure** <note>original: *Pfand*; literally a "pledge" or "pawn," here referring to a gift or talent entrusted to him by God.</note> granted to me, which is revealed daily through the bright and true light. Perhaps I may let you share in my joy, if you are kindly disposed, and you may observe therein that I still consider it my duty to serve the world and my neighbor for as long as I live, with whatever fruitfulness God may further endow me. 

Though man may strive eagerly after high things, he does not always succeed; for this reason, he should not let his established **intention** <note>original: *Intention*; in an alchemical context, this refers to the specific goal or "aim" of the chemical process.</note> falter, provided it is of worth. The city of Rome was not built in a single year, etc. This is also well known to the wise **miners**, who do not reach their goal immediately; yet, through good hope, their intention eventually turns out successfully, and they are gladdened by a single piece of ore. This is even more the case when [the topic] of the **philosophical mine** <note>original: *Philoſophiſchen Bergwercks*; a common metaphor for the alchemical process and the search for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

<summary>In this preface, Stiller introduces his work as a gift from God that he feels compelled to share with the public. He uses the metaphors of building Rome and the labor of miners to emphasize that great achievements—especially alchemical ones—require patience and persistence.</summary>

<keywords>preface, reader, divine light, persistence, mining metaphor, philosophical mine, alchemy, common good</keywords>

--- Page 101 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page's catchword "wercks," completing the metaphor of the "philosophical mine." The author, Johann Martin Stiller, discusses the trials he has faced and his preference for practical application over mere abstract theory.</meta>

# Preface <note>original: *Præfatio*</note>

work’s golden vein <note>original: *güldener Gang*; a mining term for a productive lode of ore, used here as a metaphor for alchemical success.</note> cannot be opened in one, two, three, or more years, it must not, for that reason, be considered a **non-entity** <note>original: *Non Ens*; a philosophical term for something that has no real existence.</note>. Almighty God can, according to His power, give to whomsoever He wills even in their sleep, as some will testify even to this day, should many a person be asked about an unexpected blessing of fortune.

However, for this very reason, most must endure many more obstacles, great envious falsehoods, and danger to life: which I, alas, have experienced from my youth until now, regardless of the fact that I must travel <note>original: *wallen*; implies a journey or pilgrimage.</note> wherever the Most High leads me.

I am very well aware that many books on the philosophical sciences are published in an elegant style, which are filled with mere **theory** <note>original: *Theoria*</note>. I, however, after finding and explaining things **theoretically** <note>original: *theoreticè*</note>, have sought the **practice** <note>original: *Praxin*</note>, and have now provided it to the best of my ability, though often with great difficulty. For this reason, I have also included several well-founded causes and historical accounts <note>original: *Historien*</note>, because sometimes such stories please and resonate more than [written] **experiments** <note>original: *Experimenta*</note> (writ-

<meta>catchword: ten</meta>

<summary>The author explains that alchemical success is a divine gift that may take years to manifest and often brings personal hardship and envy. He emphasizes his commitment to practical application (Praxis) over the purely abstract theories found in other scholarly books.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical practice, divine providence, philosophical mine, theory vs practice, trials of the alchemist, golden vein</keywords>

# Preface <note>original: *Præfatio*</note>

->J. M. S. C.<-

ten), the reader may therefore rest assured that wherever the nature of this writing finds an affinity <note>original: *affectionirt*; meaning the reader feels a natural inclination or attraction to the subject matter.</note>, each person should make themselves known to me. I shall conceal nothing from them, but rather wish to be helpful in all their endeavors. And although previously I did not dare to be so bold, because various important reasons prevented it, it can now occur in a spirit of good, discreet reception. Although I might have served my neighbor even better, I have been hindered in doing so; therefore, I shall apply myself from now on to serve all faithful and sincere hearts, for the sake of their health as well as their general well-being, as my highest concern. Even if many show themselves to be mocking, the work will nonetheless praise its master <note>A variation of the proverb "the work praises the craftsman," suggesting that the practical results will eventually prove the author's skill and truthfulness.</note>. Having undertaken this promise, I wish that God may grant and bestow long-lasting health, in good hope, to me and all true lovers of this **high science** <note>original: *hohen Wiſſenſchofft*; a contemporary term for alchemy or the hermetic arts.</note>.

Given at Wittgenstein <note>A county and town in Germany, known at the time as a refuge for religious dissenters and a center for esoteric thought.</note>,
the 5th of March <note>original: *Martii*</note>, 1685.

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page begins the primary text of the treatise, following the preface dated 1685. It introduces the author's critique of contemporary greed and the degradation of true alchemy.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative typographic header consisting of three rows of ornaments (fleurons, asterisks, and stylized heart-shaped patterns). Below this header, a large ornamental drop-cap "G" begins the first sentence of the text.</image-desc>

**G**ust as in the world today the noble jewel—the preservation and maintenance of health—is sought with great longing, so too is it striven for by many parties to acquire great wealth, for which the world feels the greatest passion and pleasure. It matters little whether this is pursued through right or wrong, naturally or unnaturally, as long as it serves their intent. On account of this, many good things are suppressed, while evil is permitted alongside the good. This is a state which, alas, **natural Alchemy** <note>original: *Alchymia*; the author distinguishes "natural" alchemy (the study of nature’s laws) from the fraudulent practices of his time.</note> laments! For it is falsified by coarse, earthly-minded greedy usurers and must endure the mocking tongue of **Momus** <note>In Greek mythology, Momus is the personification of mockery and unfair criticism. The author suggests alchemy is ridiculed because of the behavior of frauds.</note>. This arises because many bring themselves to shame through deceit and the conceit of their own abilities, driven by self-deluding hope. These people have never practiced any real **experience** <note>original: *experientz*</note> in the required **study** <note>original: *Studii*</note>, nor have they personally examined the **subjects** <note>original: *subjecta*; the physical materials or base substances used in alchemical operations.</note> which would show something tangible to the **devotee** <note>original: *Liebhaber*; literally "lover," a standard term for a sincere student of the Hermetic arts.</note> and be observed in a natural way. For attempting to gain knowledge solely from the writings of authors is indeed in vain; since those who have received the **Science** <note>original: *Scienz*; refers to a divinely inspired or certain knowledge of nature’s workings.</note> through God’s Spirit to perform something perfect in Nature, will [not] <note>The sentence is completed by the catchword "nicht" (not) on the following page.</note> their bitter sweat

<summary>The author begins his treatise by contrasting the noble pursuit of health with the common greed for wealth, lamenting how "natural alchemy" has been corrupted by frauds and ignoramuses who lack practical experience.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, health, wealth, Momus, experience, natural philosophy, greed, Science</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s argument from the previous page, shifting focus from the greed of false alchemists to the necessity of balancing theoretical study with practical, humble experience. It concludes the opening argument about the "Light of Knowledge."</meta>

...not to present themselves to the world so fruitlessly, even if they are already the true owners of good things; rather, they should show through their **Theory** <note>original: *Theoriam*; the conceptual framework or mental study of alchemy, often contrasted with *Praxis* or physical labor.</note> that one must also reflect a little on these matters. For certainly, their purpose is not immediately fulfilled by wishing, because more reflection, time, and the opportunity for rest are required.

Some who have never laid a hand on the work, but rather—providing only in a theoretical manner through their **speculative minds**—have written many books and brought the **light of knowledge** into darkness. This has then led many an eager, beginning **devotee** <note>original: *Liebhaber*; a standard term for a sincere student of the alchemical arts.</note> astray and caused the **Work** <note>original: *Werck*; the *Magnum Opus* or the chemical process of transformation.</note> to be mocked. I myself was once stuck in these same errors. Nonetheless, **Nature**—and much less the sincere, experienced owners of this knowledge—is not to be blamed. For they do not advise one to plunge into many vain expenses, nor do they immediately promise great wealth; instead, they point toward the very smallest things.

There, a beginning apprentice will find enough to do—indeed, even an experienced one—until he has gained the **knowledge of the science** <note>original: *Erkentnuß der Wissenschafft*; in this period, "science" referred to a systematic branch of knowledge or a mastered art rather than modern laboratory science.</note>. Then, through such guidance, **Nature** will further show how he should continue to arrange his works. For it must be taken into consideration: how can it possibly be that an inexperienced person, without any **practical skills** <note>original: *Handgriffe*; literally "hand-grips," referring to the specific manual techniques and manipulations of laboratory equipment.</note>, should undertake such great **wonders of nature** and reach his **intent** <note>original: *Intent*; his ultimate goal or purpose.</note> by force, when it is nonetheless from

...**can** be preserved by the **Spirit of God** <note>original: *Geist Gottes*</note>, and is also implanted in the one who is chosen for it. He who observes **Nature** well will find enough to do, even with great obstacles and hardships—to say nothing of the person who has never even caught the scent of it. 

It is well-known that there is no more delightful study than recognizing Nature in this way, through which the **Divine** <note>original: *Göttliche Divinũ*; the author uses both German and Latin to emphasize the sacredness of this knowledge.</note> is praised and good is done for the world and our neighbors. For even if all vain hope of wealth were withheld by God, the great benefit remains: to regain and maintain that most noble treasure, human **health**. 

For the wise **Solomon** <note>original: *Salom.*; King Solomon was historically revered by alchemists as a master of natural secrets and the hidden properties of the world.</note> himself directs us to **minerals** <note>original: *Mineralia*</note>, stones, and **plants** <note>original: *Vegetabilia*</note>, showing that within them lies the help intended for man by God. In this, the **animal kingdom** <note>original: *Animale*</note> must not be forgotten, for from its subjects many excellent **medicines** <note>original: *Medicinen*</note> are made, which have been found with great benefit and praise to heal human infirmities. 

But just as animal, vegetable, or mineral matter cannot be used for benefit on its own, because it is burdened and locked away by many clinging **accidents** <term>accidents: in traditional philosophy and alchemy, these are the external, non-essential qualities of a substance (like dirt or outer husks) that must be removed to reach the pure, healing "essence" within.</term>; for this, **separation** is required, which occurs through **alchemy** <note>original: *Alchymie*</note>. Through this, one thing is driven into another; this is called an artistic change or **transmutation** <note>original: *transmutation*</note>, and it cannot happen otherwise 

<summary>The author argues that the true purpose of studying nature and alchemy is the praise of God and the restoration of human health, rather than the pursuit of wealth. <meta>This page continues from the previous page's discussion on how the hidden virtues of nature—whether animal, vegetable, or mineral—cannot be accessed except through specific transformative processes. ->✾ * ✾<-

**can** occur than through **Alchemy**, which provides a heartfelt benefit and profit to whoever knows how to practice it correctly. For on one hand, the word Alchemy can be understood as the **Art of Fire** <term>Art of Fire: *Feurkunst*, a historical term for the chemical and metallurgical practices involving furnaces</term>, though there are several other interpretations and meanings for this name. If we move from **Mining** <term>mining: the extraction of ores from the earth</term> to **Smelting** <term>smelting: the industrial process of melting ore to extract base metals</term>, and we wish to make use of a **Mineral** <term>mineral: any naturally occurring inorganic substance</term>—which is sometimes rich in value but poor in appearance—through proper **Foundry-work** <term>foundry-work: the refinement and casting of metals</term>, a transformation will certainly occur. By using **additives** <term>additives: *additamenta*, substances like flux added to a melt to separate the metal from the waste material</term>, the purity is brought out by the fire, which is then commonly called **Metal**. Thus, it has been changed from ore into metal, a process that happens through nothing other than **Alchemy** <note>original: *Alchymiam*</note>. Furthermore, this metal is divided for various uses, namely for medicine, coinage, and human adornment, which is then transmuted into other forms. All of this is credited to the power of Alchemy, because not a single craftsman who wishes to perform something useful with fire can do without it, as both daily and ancient experience shows us. If one further examines **Chemistry**, it sufficiently explains the skill of decomposing minerals and metals—specifically, how every metal or mineral is resolved into its **principles** <term>principles: *principia*, the fundamental components or "elements" that make up a substance in early chemistry</term>, and how from each principle the **pure is separated from the impure** <note>original: *purum ab impuro*</note>.

It is to be wished that those lovers of true Alchemy, and especially noble **Potentates** <term>potentates: rulers or monarchs with great power</term>, would examine these things with their own hands and treat just one **subject** <term>subject: *subjectum*, the physical material or matter being acted upon in a chemical process</term> for natural observation. They, like others—both those who have passed and those living in secret—would then rejoice in being numbered among the possessors of good and perfect gifts.

->⚜ ✳ ⚜<-

The art of transforming metals, according to my experience, could satisfy my friends to such an extent that I might fill a large volume, perhaps even massive **folios**, if I intended to uselessly employ the **sayings** <note>original: *dictorum*; authoritative quotes from ancient sources</note> of other people or lengthy, futile processes. Instead, I shall demonstrate on only a few sheets whether it is possible to improve one metal into another, and what properties the so-called **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> actually possesses in its quality. Furthermore, I will show that it is also possible to prepare a **universal medicine** and thereby ward off all diseases.

However, so that it may be understood how one actually attains this **mystery** <note>original: *Mysterio*</note>, it first requires other preliminary works. These have a broad scope and do not reach the philosophical goal immediately, but they provide good guidance when such works are performed with **observation**, so that one comes **step by step** <note>original: *gradatim*</note> closer to the right end.

Therefore, let us take up the **principles** <note>original: *Principia*</note> commonly used at this time: namely **Salt**, **Sulfur** <term>Δ: the alchemical symbol for Sulfur, one of the three prime substances</term>, and **Mercury** <term>☿: the alchemical symbol for Mercury/Quicksilver</term> of the mineral kingdom. It will undoubtedly be known to everyone who has worked even a little with these named substances in the common way—especially the deceitful **Sophists** <note>Sophists: a derogatory term for "puffers" or fraudulent alchemists who lacked true philosophical understanding</note>—that Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury readily accept one another, especially when they **prepare** <note>original: *præpariren*</note> the **Mercury** <term>♎︎: the symbol for the balance/Libra, here likely referring to the "balance" of the substance or a specific distillation of Antimony</term>. Yet, in doing so, they pay too little attention, but rather to...

To this alone belongs the power of resolution, and to no other thing. This philosophical field can now be viewed and tasted by every seeker through me, and they shall find more pleasure in seeing it than in the many errors floating about the world. For it is well known that when the earth has carried the [offspring] for a long time...

<meta>This page continues the philosophical justification for metallic transmutation. While the previous page focused on the physical result of coloring glass with a "fixed Sulfur," the author now explains the underlying natural theory: that "opening" a metal through decay (putrefaction) allows it to be changed into a higher form, just as life arises from decay in the animal and vegetable kingdoms.</meta>

->⁂ * ⁂<-

<margin>|</margin>Improvement of the metals can be brought
<margin>|</margin>about. And thus, the possibility of improving one metal through
<margin>|</margin>another may be given approval and belief. Because it is naturally acceptable;
for if a true <term>opening <note>original: "Auffschliessung"; the alchemical process of breaking down a substance's hard outer form to release its inner essence.</note></term> of every thing
occurs, why should a transformation
not also follow? For the opening is precisely
the <term>putrefaction <note>original: "putrefaction"; the stage of "death" or decomposition in the alchemical process, which was believed to be necessary before a new, perfected form could arise.</note></term>, and through it,
changing things always follow. For it is
seen in the animal kingdom how, through
decay, one <term>species</term> <term>transmutes <note>Changes from one type of being into another.</note></term>
into another, and yet the vitality always remains
effectively present according to the nature of the condition;
just as the <term>menstruum <note>original: "Menstrum"; a term used here not in a biological sense, but to describe a universal solvent or life-giving fluid that dissolves and nourishes.</note></term>, as one might call the moisture
of the vegetable kingdom, brings forth living
little creatures through decay. Why, then,
should not also a pure <term>Mercury <note>original: "Quecksilber"; the fluid, transformative principle of all metals.</note></term>, when
combined with its <term>Salt</term> and fire-burning <term>fixed Sulfur <note>original: "fixen Schweffel"; Sulfur represents the active, masculine, and "oily" principle that gives metal its color and combustible nature.</note></term>,
be capable of an opening of the gold?
For just as the other two kingdoms [animal and vegetable] have their
<term>radical moisture <note>original: "WurzelFeuchtigkeit"; the fundamental, internal liquid that sustains the life and form of a substance.</note></term> and adhering <term>seed <note>original: "sperma"; the generative essence or "blueprint" of a thing.</note></term>;
so this [the mineral kingdom] also has the power to bring its metal
back into the first active principle, into the same
<term>substance</term> as it is itself. Here it must
be understood—not as many have worked,
and as some still think: that the <term>subject <note>original: "subjectum"; the raw material or starting point of the alchemical work.</note></term> which
one takes in hand must be <term>separated</term>
into sulfur, salt, and mercury in the common manner;

when there is a fire of **putrefaction** <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "Faulung"; the process of breaking down a substance through gentle, moist heat, often compared to digestion.</note> through **manure** <note>original: "Mi-"; likely "Mist" (manure), a common source of steady, gentle heat in early chemistry.</note> or earth. <meta>This page continues the discussion from page 32 regarding the different types of fire in alchemical practice. <image-desc>A decorative headpiece featuring a central star ornament flanked by two floral or foliate motifs.</image-desc>

is distinguished, because different properties are attributed to the **Philosopher’s Fire** <term>Philosophisches Feur</term> <note>original: "Philosoph: Feur"; in alchemy, this is not a literal flame but a subtle, internal heat or spiritual essence within matter.</note>; namely, that it moistens—indeed, that it is a **moist, cold fire**. This fire is also called an **incombustible eternal light** <note>original: "unverbrennliches ewiges Liecht"; a poetic name for the quintessence or the divine spark hidden within material bodies.</note>, the **little spark of bodies**, and is thus distinguished from the **eating fire** or those **devouring waters** <note>original: "freßenden Wassern"; likely referring to corrosive acids or strong solvents that destroy the form of a substance.</note>, which indeed consist of **sulfur** <term>Schweffel</term> <note>original: "Schweffel"; representing the active, combustible, and soul-principle in alchemy.</note>, but of a sulfur that is far too porous. Likewise, **common fire** burns and dries unless one protects the bodies with water; it spoils the Philosophers' fire—its assistance and essence—if it were not maintained by its own water. This is the difference: that elementary fire loosens and makes porous, while the Philosophers' fire **thickens** <note>original: "angedicket"; refers to the process of coagulation or the "fixing" of a volatile substance.</note>. The seat and fodder of common fire is in the air, but that of the Philosophers is in the water; therefore, **all that is above is equal to that which is below** <note>A famous axiom from the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, suggesting a correspondence between the macrocosm (the universe) and the microcosm (the alchemical vessel or the human soul).</note>.

Furthermore, let the steadfast lover of this high secret hear of the distress and error in which many who have undertaken the **Work** <note>original: "Werck"; the Great Work or "Magnum Opus" of alchemy.</note> are stuck, because the Philosophers have made mention of the **putrefaction** <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "putrefaction"; the essential first stage of the work where the matter must rot or decompose, often symbolized by the color black.</note> of the first 40 days. In this stage, the **blackness** <note>original: "Schwärtze"; the "Nigredo" stage, which signifies the death of the old form before rebirth.</note> should show itself; if it does not, the work would not have been properly begun. Because of this, many become saddened, doubt the right **matter** <term>Matery</term> <note>original: "Matery"; the base substance or "Prima Materia" required for the transformation.</note>, and leave the whole work standing. The Philosophers have always and at all times hidden the truth with **Cabalistic and metaphorical speeches** <note>original: "Cabal- und Metaphorische Reden"; refers to the use of symbolic, coded language (the "Language of the Birds") to keep secrets from the uninitiated.</note>...

<summary>This page defines the "Philosopher’s Fire" as a moist, cold, and incombustible light, contrasting it with common fire and corrosive solvents. It also warns practitioners not to be discouraged if the "blackness" of putrefaction does not appear exactly as described in the cryptic metaphors of older alchemical texts.</summary>
